The ~17% universe widening genuinely de-calibrates the gate; §4.4's 'preserve the historical fire-date set, do not tune' and Q5's ban on cutover while capit_fired=true are the correct constraints. §7's refusal to accept 'ETL bugs not relevant' also survives reading the now-located original QA doc: its Phan 4 item 5 states max_bad_records=10 with NO table scoping, so Q8 remains genuinely open."}, "independent_recompute": "Re-ran all cited BQ aggregates. Confirmed exactly: superset 738.201 rows / 0 orphans; n_prune PIT 140/180/233 at 2014-06-30, 2018-06-29, 2026-06-15; n_rule 273 and intersect 230 at 2026-06-15; threshold sweep 5e8->336, 1e9->273, 2e9->220 with recall 193/233=82,8%. Then ran the DECISIVE extra query the finding did not run: substituting the literal production filter `Volume_3M_P50*Price/Inflation_7 > 1e9` yields intersect == n_prune exactly at all three dates (recall 100,0%), proving prune is a perfect subset of the liquidity filter and that the 97-99% figure measures Taylor's own formula noise, not curation information content. Also measured Inflation_7 directly (2026=1.0, 2014=0.4186-0.4501) to establish the algebraic identity, and extended the threshold sweep to 2018/2022 to test the n=1 plateau (it holds).", "killer_objection": "The recall statistic cannot do the work the finding assigns to it. Because ticker_prune membership IS (hit_ticker_list AND the liquidity filter), and B3 IS that same liquidity filter re-expressed, 'prune is a subset of rule' is true by construction — I measured it at exactly 100,0% using the production formula. It would be equally 100% if hit_ticker_list were a hand-picked list of the 453 best-performing names in backtest history. Therefore it provides no evidence whatsoever that bq_admin's curation is uninformative, and the doc-goc §5 concern (that curation subtracts genuinely bad names: warning-list, qualified-audit, restricted) remains completely untested. Everything measured is consistent with curation being highly informative subtraction.", "recommended_reruns": ["Replace the recall test with one that has actual power: sample the 43 rule-only names at 2026-06-15 and the ~29 at 2014-06-30, and check them against external quality evidence unavailable to the liquidity rule (HOSE/HNX warning & control lists, qualified/adverse audit opinions, delisting within 12 months, FSCORE/ROE_Min3Y distribution vs the prune-included cohort). If rule-only names are materially worse ex-post, curation WAS informative and B3 alone is insufficient.", "Restate §2.2 honestly: report recall of the exact production formula (100%) and label the 97-99% as formula-drift between the prune filter and the proposed median-60 spec — not as evidence about curation.", "Re-derive the look-ahead magnitude against the denominator the code actually uses (543 ever, no time predicate) rather than the non-reproducing 459; quote 2,7x at 2018 and note the 459-vs-381 drift as primary evidence for non-reproducibility.", "Keep §3.2's mandated first implementation step (recompute the recall table with self-computed median-60-session Price*Volume) — but treat any recall drop as expected formula divergence, NOT as a signal to re-tune B3 back toward 1e9, which would be exactly the CAGR-free tuning trap §8.4 warns about.", "Close §8.1 BEFORE P2/P4: publish the 43 rule-only names with ADV and 8L/FSCORE for human review, since they cluster just above the 1B/day floor.", "Send bq_admin the §7/Q8 question — the located original QA doc does not scope max_bad_records=10 to any table, so the 'ETL bugs are prune-only' assumption is still unverified and B8 remains mandatory."], "summary": "Every number in this finding is real — I reproduced all of them exactly from live BigQuery, including the 0-orphan superset, the PIT prune counts at three dates, and the full threshold sweep. But the headline inference is void. Inflation_7 is a 2026-anchored deflator, which makes the proposed B3 rule algebraically identical to the production prune row-filter already hardcoded in filter.json:18 — so 'recall 97-99%' is not an independent reconstruction of bq_admin's curation, it is the prune filter measuring itself. Running the literal production formula gives recall of exactly 100,0% at all three dates, because prune = hit_ticker_list INTERSECT liquidity_filter and therefore 'prune is a subset of the rule' is true by construction no matter what hit_ticker_list contains. The finding uses this to declare the doc-goc §5 worry ('curation may filter out warning-list / qualified-audit names, so blind replacement degrades the universe') as 'not supported by the data' — but §5's worry is precisely about subtraction, and a test that can only observe subtraction has zero power against it. I refute that claim, not the project. The plan's actual direction survives independently and comfortably: circular selection bias is confirmed by bq_admin's own answer (hit_ticker_list derives from profile_hit.csv, i.e. backtest deals), non-reproducibility is confirmed (IVS 0 to 1.622 rows in 8 days, and I watched n_union drift 459 to 381 myself), look-ahead is real and in fact larger than quoted (2,7x not 2,3x, since the D1 pattern has no time predicate at all), and the CAPIT breadth-denominator hazard is verified in code at golive_recommend_v23.py:655/96/430. Credit where due: the self-flagged n=1 sweep risk resolves in Taylor's favour when I extend it to 2018 and 2022, the refusal to accept the 'ETL bugs are irrelevant' assumption is vindicated by the original QA text, and §8.4's warning about self-tuning a universe for a system whose history is known is exactly the right instinct — which is why §2.2's overclaim needs correcting before it hardens into fleet consensus. Recommend proceeding with Q1/Q3/Q4/Q5 on the confirmed grounds, and rewriting §2.2 so that no future reader cites 97-99% as proof that curation was uninformative."}

Every published number reproduces exactly from my own BigQuery pull of the production SQL — 83→76 fire days, the same 7 lost dates, zero false fires, the same single grind flip, the same live 0.5096→0.4960, the same {HVT} pool delta. The gate 0.31 is not a tuned magic number: br250 is quantised to 1/250, the qualifying window is exactly the two adjacent grid values 0.3080/0.3120, and every gate inside it produces identical results, so 0.31 is an entire equivalence class rather than a fragile point; the only judgment made was preferring zero false fires over one, a criterion declared before measurement. No look-ahead: universe_pit is rule-built from point-in-time raw columns and the quality view does not touch membership. The fail-closed staleness check is correctly directional (it can only over-block, never under-block, for the lag case it targets). Splitting breadth and pool into two switches is right, and pinning the pool is well justified — HVT is a genuine ADV trap (2.063 tỷ one-day turnover vs 0.196 tỷ ADV) that would have added a real order and diluted an in-flight basket. Two residual gaps worth logging but neither falsifies the claim: the VND cost of the 7 lost days was never backtested (the CAPIT harness still runs the old 0.30/prune trigger), and capit_grind's positional lookback remains vulnerable to a mid-window universe_pit hole that the last-day staleness check cannot see."}
